There is no indication of any bit position as high as 58 for any field, including CTI. Therefore, a bit_offset of 58 for the CTI field would be invalid. This is supported by the documented address offset (0x34) and reset value (0x0000 0000) for BKP_CSR, as well as the defined bit positions within the 16-bit register range.

It is not read as 1 or 0 (reads as 0), so it is not a read-write field. The BKP CSR register (BKP_CSR) has different fields (ASOE, CCO, CAL, TPAL, TPE, etc.) and does not contain a CTI field. Therefore, a CTI field in BKP_CSR with read-write access is not correct. See BKP_CR details (offset 0x30) and CTI description, and BKP_CSR field listing for comparison.

I located the EXTI Event Mask Register (EXTI_EMR) documentation in the RM0041 datasheet. It shows:
- EXTI_EMR is at address offset 0x04, with a reset value of 0x0000 0000. This confirms the register location we’re examining. 
- The register contains MR17:0 bits, i.e., MRx for x = 0..17, where MR0 is the least significant bit. This means MR0 sits at bit position 0, not 40. 
- In other sections, the same MRx description is shown as MRx: Event mask on line x, aligning MR0 with bit 0. 

Conclusion: The given bit_offset value of 40 for field MR0 is not correct; MR0 resides at bit 0 of EXTI_EMR, and the EMR supports MR0 through MR17 (lines 0–17). Therefore, the statement is false.

```json

This strongly suggests TRx bits in RTSR/FTSR are read/write, not write-only.

I checked the FSMC RM0041 documentation for the BCRx (bank control) register. The CBURSTRW field is defined as a single-bit field located at bit 19 (bit 19 CBURSTRW), meaning its bit width is 1. The documentation consistently shows this field as a single bit (and its reset/value behavior is indicated as 0 in the tables). Therefore, the provided value of 15 for the bit_width is not correct.

The extmod field in the FSMC BCRx register is defined as bit 14. In the FSMC_BCRx bit-field table, EXTMOD is shown at bit 14 (with possible values 0 or 1 depending on mode) and the surrounding bits span 0–31 for the 32-bit register. A bit offset of 83 would be out of range for a 32-bit register, so the provided value cannot be true.

I located the FSMC reference material (RM0041) and found that the EXTMOD bit is defined as bit 14 in the FSMC_BCRx register, labeled "Extended mode enable." This confirms the field exists within BCR2 (and other BCRx registers) and is a configurable bit rather than a reserved one. The manual further states that when extended mode is enabled, the timing configuration can be split between read/write pathways, indicating the bit is meant to be set/read by software. This supports the notion that the extmod field has read/write access. Specifically, EXTMOD is described as an enable bit, and examples show it being set to 0 or 1 to activate/deactivate extended mode.   

```json
{
  "is_true": true,
  "confidence_score": 0.92

The value 66 is not a valid bit width for a field in this register, so the statement that the bit width equals 66 is false. The documentation explicitly indicates EXTMOD at bit 14 and shows it as a 1-bit field (1-bit width by virtue of being a single bit).

I found the FSMC documentation (RM0041) which defines the bit fields for the BCRx registers. In Table 103 of the FSMC_BCRx bit fields, bit 6 is FACCEN. Since BCR2 is one of the BCRx registers, FACCEN is located at bit offset 6 for BCR2 as well. This matches the requested field and offset.  

```json
{
  "is_true": true,
  "confidence_score": 1.0
}
```


Reasoning:
- The FSMC_BCRx (e.g., BCR2) register contains the FACCEN field at bit 6, which is described as the Flash Access Enable bit. The datasheet indicates a value of 0x1 for enabling this feature, implying the bit is writable (i.e., not a fixed read-only status) and can be programmed to enable flash access. 
- The documentation also notes that if FACCEN is reset (i.e., cleared), the FSMC will generate an AHB error when accessing the NOR flash bank, which further confirms that FACCEN is a software-controlled, writable bit rather than a read-only status indicator. 
- In the BCRx field diagrams, the bit fields are shown as rw (read/write) fields, reinforcing that these bits are programmable by software. 

Conclusion: The field access is not read-only; therefore the provided fact (access = read-only) is false.
